

================================================================================
FILE: README.md
================================================================================

# Secondary Condition Finder

A claim-preparation research aid for VA disability claims. It covers two
legally distinct paths and keeps them separate, because they need different
evidence.

- **Secondary** (38 CFR 3.310): given a service-connected condition, returns
  ranked conditions to explore, each with reasoning, evidence gaps, and
  citations. Needs a nexus opinion.
- **Presumptive** (38 CFR 3.307 and 3.309): given where and when you served,
  returns the exposure presumptions that may apply. No nexus opinion needed.

Covers the PACT Act burn pit list and the Agent Orange list. See
`docs/presumptive-track.md` for scope and what is deliberately excluded.

**This is not medical or legal advice.** It does not diagnose conditions,
determine eligibility, or estimate ratings. Every suggestion is a possibility
to discuss with a qualified provider or an accredited representative.

## Status

Phase 1: web app with no live VA data. See `docs/product-spec.md` for scope and
`docs/phase-plan.md` for the full roadmap.

All source citations currently ship as **unverified**. They have not been
confirmed by a qualified reviewer and are displayed as starting points for your
own verification, not as authority.

## Stack

Next.js (App Router), TypeScript, Tailwind, Zod, Vitest. The knowledge base is
JSON in version control so every change to a condition mapping or a citation
arrives as a reviewable diff. SQLite backs local event logging.

## Getting started

```bash
npm install
npm run dev
```

Open http://localhost:3000. Try `/scenarios` for repeatable demo walkthroughs.

## Commands

| Command | What it does |
|---------|--------------|
| `npm run dev` | Development server |
| `npm run build` | Production build |
| `npm test` | Vitest suite |
| `npm run test:watch` | Vitest in watch mode |
| `npm run typecheck` | `tsc --noEmit` |
| `npm run lint` | ESLint |

## Configuration

| Variable | Default | Purpose |
|----------|---------|---------|
| `EVENT_LOG_SQLITE_PATH` | unset | Path to a SQLite file for recommendation event logs. When unset, events are written as structured stdout lines instead. |

## Layout

```
data/conditions/       Condition catalog, one JSON file per body system
data/knowledge-base/   Secondary mappings, one JSON file per primary
data/presumptives/     Exposure categories, locations, and date windows
data/demo-scenarios.json
src/lib/catalog/       Zod schema, loader, lookup. Condition identity.
src/lib/kb/            Zod schema, loader, mapping helpers
src/lib/engine/        Pure recommendation logic, no I/O
src/lib/presumptive/   Zod schema, loader, service-history matcher
src/lib/log/           Event logging behind an EventLogger interface
src/app/               App Router pages
src/components/        Presentational components
tests/                 Vitest
docs/                  Product spec, catalog and presumptive scope, planning
```

Routes:

- `/` chooser
- `/secondary` workflow overview plus:
  - `/secondary/diagnosed`
  - `/secondary/service-connected`
  - `/secondary/ratings`
  - `/secondary/upload` (optional checklist flag only; no file parsing or medical interpretation)
  - `/secondary/suggestions`
  - `/secondary/evidence`
- `/results` legacy query-based secondary results (kept for scenario/demo compatibility)
- `/presumptive` and `/presumptive/results`
- `/scenarios`

Wizard notes:

- Later secondary steps are route-guarded and require valid prior-step query state.
- Back links preserve prior selections so users can revise choices without restarting.
- Suggestions and evidence pages require an explicit upload-step choice (`documentsShared`)
  even though file upload itself is optional.

## Coverage

191 conditions across 14 body systems are in the catalog.

- 191 have version-controlled mapping files.
- 17 are published (`draft: false`) and active in recommendations.
- 174 are draft placeholders (`draft: true`) and excluded from recommendation output.

Intake labels unpublished conditions as "no mappings yet" rather than implying
full coverage. See `docs/condition-catalog.md`.


## Adding a condition

Two steps, and they are separate on purpose.

1. **Catalog.** Add it to the matching `data/conditions/<body-system>.json`
   with a unique id, name, aliases, and a rating citation if you have a real
   one. This makes the condition selectable.
2. **Mappings.** Create `data/knowledge-base/<id>.json` where the filename
   matches the `id`. Each suggestion needs `conditionId`, `rank`, `prevalence`,
   `rationale`, at least one evidence gap, and at least one source reference
   with `verified: false`. This makes it return results.

Run `npm test` to check the integrity invariants, including that every
`conditionId` resolves to a catalog entry. The app also validates these
references at load time, so drift fails fast before recommendations run.

### Draft mappings

A mapping may ship as `draft: true` with an empty `suggestions` array and a
`todo` checklist. Drafts are validated and version controlled but never appear
in the search, scenario, or recommendation UI flows, so unfinished research
cannot reach a veteran.

Contribution rules for humans and agents alike are in
`.github/copilot-instructions.md`. The citation rules there are not optional.

## Privacy

No accounts, no personally identifying information, and no intake free text is
persisted. Event logs record resolved condition names, counts, and a timestamp
only, which is enough to explain why a given set of suggestions was produced.

      "conditionId": "myelofibrosis",
      "name": "Myelofibrosis",
      "group": "Blood and bone marrow",
      "qualifier": null
    }
  ],
  "sourceReferences": [
    {
      "citation": "PACT Act of 2022 (Public Law 117-168)",
      "note": "Statute establishing these presumptions.",
      "url": "https://www.va.gov/disability/eligibility/hazardous-materials-exposure/specific-environmental-hazards/",
      "verified": false
    },
    {
      "citation": "VA, Presumptive Service Connection Eligibility (updated 4 February 2026)",
      "note": "VA reference sheet listing conditions, locations, and date windows.",
      "url": "https://www.va.gov/files/2026-03/presumptive-service-connection-information_0.pdf",
      "verified": false
    },
    {
      "citation": "38 CFR 3.307",
      "note": "General presumptive service connection requirements, including exposure and manifestation periods.",
      "url": "https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/38/3.307",
      "verified": false
    }
  ]
}

# Subject Matter Expert (SME) Authoring Guide

## Welcome to the Secondary Condition Finder Knowledge Base
This repository maps primary service-connected conditions to potential secondary conditions under **38 CFR 3.310**. 

Your job is to take `draft: true` JSON files and populate them with accurate clinical rationales and legal citations.

### The JSON Structure
Every draft file lives in `data/knowledge-base/`. To publish a file, you must provide:

1. **Suggestions Array:** Add 1 or more secondary conditions.
   - `conditionId`: Must exactly match a file in `data/conditions/`.
   - `rank`: Starts at 1. Lower number = higher likelihood.
   - `prevalence`: "commonly-explored", "sometimes-explored", or "occasionally-explored".
   - `rationale`: Plain-text medical explanation (e.g., "Altered gait from knee strain shifts biomechanical load to the contralateral hip...").
   - `evidenceGaps`: Array of strings detailing what a veteran needs (e.g., "Current hip diagnosis", "IMO linking gait alteration").
2. **Source References:** Between **1 and 3 maximum** sources per suggestion.
   - `citation`: E.g., "38 CFR 3.310(a)".
   - `note`: Plain text explanation of the rule.
   - `verified`: **MUST REMAIN FALSE**. (This flags it for final legal review).

### How to Promote a File
Once you have filled out the JSON and emptied the `todo: []` array:
1. Save the file.
2. Run the validation script: `node scripts/promote-kb-batch.js <condition-id>`
3. Commit and push your changes.

# Accessibility Audit & Compliance Report (WCAG 2.1 AA)

## Overview
This document outlines the accessibility standards, automated validation rules, keyboard navigation protocols, and manual screen reader pass criteria for the **Secondary Condition Finder for VA Claims** application.

---

## 1. Compliance Standard: WCAG 2.1 Level AA
The application targets WCAG 2.1 Level AA compliance, ensuring usability for veterans relying on screen readers (NVDA, VoiceOver), keyboard-only navigation, and high-contrast display modes.

---

## 2. Key Accessibility Requirements & Implementation
- **Semantic HTML & Landmarks:** Every view utilizes proper landmark elements (`<main>`, `<nav>`, `<header>`, `<footer>`) and hierarchical headings (`<h1>` through `<h4>`).
- **Form Labelling & ARIA:** All form controls, checkboxes, and inputs are explicitly associated with descriptive labels via `<label for="...">` or `aria-labelledby`.
- **Keyboard Traversal:** Interactive elements (buttons, links, inputs, accordions) are fully focusable in logical tab order with visible focus indicators.
- **Color Contrast:** Text elements maintain a minimum contrast ratio of 4.5:1 against their background (Tailwind `gray-900` on white/light backgrounds).

---

## 3. Automated Test Verification
Automated tests in `tests/accessibility-ux-reliability.test.ts` validate:
1. Presence of primary heading structures (`<h1>`) on core routes.
2. Form input labelling and explicit accessible names.
3. ARIA roles and landmark presence.

---

## 4. Manual Screen Reader & Keyboard Navigation Protocol
| Test Case | Tool / Method | Expected Result | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Tab Traversal** | Physical Keyboard (Tab / Shift+Tab) | Focus moves logically through interactive intake elements without traps. | Verified |
| **Screen Reader Announcement** | NVDA (Windows) / VoiceOver (macOS) | Form inputs announce labels, error states, and result counts correctly. | Verified |
| **High Contrast Mode** | OS High Contrast / Dark Mode | All text and badges remain legible and meet contrast thresholds. | Verified |

## 5. Privacy & Zero PII Policy
- **Zero PII:** Ensures zero collection or persistence of veteran personal data during accessibility and screen-reader interactions.

FILE: docs/condition-catalog.md

# Condition catalog

## Three different things

The app models three concepts that are easy to conflate. Keep them apart.

| Concept | Question it answers | Authority | Location |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Catalog** | Does this condition exist, what is it called, how is severity rated | 38 CFR Part 4 (VASRD) | `data/conditions/` |
| **Secondary mappings** | What might this condition have caused or worsened | 38 CFR 3.310 | `data/knowledge-base/` |
| **Presumptives** | What does service in a place and time presume | 38 CFR 3.307, 3.309 | `data/presumptives/` |

A rating schedule citation says **how VA evaluates severity**. It is not
evidence of service connection and must never be presented as such. A condition
appearing in the catalog means only that it exists and is ratable.

## Structure

One file per body system, 14 systems plus a carried-over file. 191 conditions.

```json
{
  "id": "sleep-apnea",
  "name": "Sleep Apnea (Obstructive, Central, Mixed)",
  "aliases": ["obstructive", "central", "mixed"],
  "bodySystem": "Respiratory System",
  "ratingSchedule": [
    { "citation": "38 CFR 4.97", "note": "...", "verified": false }
  ],
  "reviewNotes": []
}
```

The catalog is the single source of condition identity. Mapping files carry
only `conditionId` references, so a display name cannot drift between two
files. Referential integrity is enforced by test: every id referenced by a
mapping must exist in the catalog. The same check also runs at load time before
recommendations are computed.

## Coverage

- 191 conditions across 14 body systems
- 185 carry a rating schedule citation, all `verified: false`
- 6 carry none. Those are entries the knowledge base already referenced that
  the source list omitted: `hip-strain`, `ankle-strain`,
  `contralateral-knee-condition`, `diabetic-retinopathy`, `insomnia`, and
  `substance-use-disorder`. No citation was supplied and none was inferred.
  Each carries a `reviewNotes` entry saying so.
- 191 conditions have authored knowledge-base files: 17 published (active in recommendations) and 174 draft placeholders (excluded from UI flows until completed).
  catalog. Intake states this plainly rather than implying full coverage.

## Known gaps in the source list

Worth resolving before the catalog is treated as complete.

- **No hip or ankle joint entries** in the musculoskeletal section, despite
  entries for spine, shoulder, elbow, wrist, knee, and foot. Both are common
  compensatory secondary claims.
- **No insomnia** entry, despite narcolepsy and restless leg syndrome.
- **No substance use disorder** entry, despite a full mental disorders section.
- **No diabetic retinopathy** entry, despite retinal detachment and macular
  degeneration.
- **Cerebral palsy** is listed. Congenital and developmental defects are
  generally barred from service connection by 38 CFR 3.303(c). Whether it
  belongs here needs a decision.
- **`contralateral-knee-condition`** is not a distinct diagnosis. It is the
  same condition on the opposite side. Laterality should become an attribute
  rather than a separate catalog entry.

## Citation status

Every rating citation ships `verified: false`. The source list arrived with a
self-declared "Hallucination Check" asserting the citations were accurate. A
generator asserting its own correctness is not review. Treat these exactly like
every other unverified citation in this repo until a qualified human checks
them.

One to check first: **fibromyalgia** was supplied as
`38 CFR 4.71a / 38 CFR 4.88b`. Fibromyalgia is rated under 4.71a. The 4.88b
reference belongs to the infectious disease and immune disorder schedule and
may be a carryover from chronic fatigue syndrome. Recorded as supplied, flagged
here rather than silently changed.

## Adding a condition

Add it to the body system file that fits. Give it a unique kebab-case `id`, a
display `name`, any unambiguous `aliases`, and a rating citation if you have a
real one. If you do not have one, leave `ratingSchedule` empty and add a
`reviewNotes` entry explaining why. Never invent a citation.

Aliases must be unambiguous. A test rejects an alias claimed by two conditions,
or one that collides with another condition's name or id.

FILE: docs/event-logging-operations.md

# Event Logging: Operational Guidance (SQLite vs. Memory Backends)

This document outlines the operational characteristics, deployment trade-offs, and security/privacy boundaries for event logging backends in the Secondary Condition Finder application.

---

## 1. Backend Comparison Matrix

| Operational Dimension | SQLite Logger (`events.db`) | In-Memory Logger (`MemoryEventLogger`) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Durability** | Persistent across server restarts and VM reboots. | Ephemeral (cleared on process restart or container scale-down). |
| **Concurrency** | Handles concurrent read/write operations via SQLite locking mechanisms. | Thread/Async safe within a single Node.js runtime instance. |
| **Storage Footprint** | Disk-backed (configured via `EVENT_LOG_SQLITE_PATH`). | RAM-backed; subject to array size limits or rolling ring-buffers. |
| **Primary Use Case** | Local development, audit logging, analytics persistence on single-node Azure VMs. | Unit/Integration testing, stateless serverless / containerized scaling where local disk is ephemeral. |

---

## 2. Privacy & Compliance Posture (Non-Negotiable)

Regardless of whether SQLite or Memory logging is active, both backends adhere to strict privacy rules:
1. **Zero Free Text Persistence:** Intake free text, unstructured user notes, and clinical narratives are **never** persisted to event logs.
2. **PII Masking:** Social security numbers, patient names, and raw medical diagnoses are automatically rejected or stripped by `sanitizeAndValidateEventPayload`.
3. **Audit Trail:** Only structured telemetry events (e.g., step progression, catalog category selection, error telemetry) are recorded.

---

## 3. Production Deployment Recommendations

- **Single-Node Azure VM:** Use **SQLite** backed by the persistent OS disk (`Premium LRS`). Ensure `.env.local` points `EVENT_LOG_SQLITE_PATH` to a secured directory (`./data/events.db`) with restricted file permissions (`chmod 600`).
- **Stateless / Horizontal Scaling:** If deploying across multiple load-balanced containers or serverless functions without shared persistent volumes, use **Memory Logger** or an external centralized telemetry sink to prevent split-brain SQLite locking issues.

4. **Publish & Test:** Run `npm test` and `npm run build` to ensure zero Zod schema regression, then commit and push in reviewable batches.

# Knowledge Base Mapping PR Data QA Checklist

Before merging any pull request modifying files under `data/knowledge-base/`, verify the following items:

- [ ] **Citation Presence:** Every suggestion includes valid 38 CFR regulatory references (e.g., `38 CFR 3.310`, `38 CFR 4.71a`).
- [ ] **Unverified Flag Enforcement:** All citations explicitly include `"verified": false` to indicate they require professional medical/legal validation.
- [ ] **No Placeholders:** Zero instances of `TODO`, `TBD`, or placeholder text exist if `draft: false`.
- [ ] **Rank Continuity:** Suggestion ranks are strictly unique, gapless, and start at `1` (`1, 2, 3...`).
- [ ] **Non-Self Reference:** No condition references its own ID as a secondary condition (`conditionId !== primaryId`).
- [ ] **Evidence Gap Depth:** Each suggestion outlines at least one actionable clinical evidence gap or diagnostic requirement.
- [ ] **Test Suite Green:** Running `npm test` passes all 83+ automated tests successfully without schema validation errors.

# Secondary Condition Finder Phase Plan

## Purpose

This plan defines a narrow, defensible delivery path that proves product value without requiring sensitive live VA data up front. It is organized into three phases so the team can build a working product, validate usefulness, and then pursue only the minimum required production API access.

## Product Guardrails (applies to all phases)

- The tool supports claim-preparation research and organization only.
- The tool does **not** diagnose conditions or provide legal advice.
- Outputs are framed as “possible secondary conditions to explore,” not determinations.
- Recommendations must include rationale and evidence-gap prompts.
- Data handling must follow least-privilege and minimum-necessary principles.

---

## Phase 1: MVP & Core Evaluation Engine

### Core Deliverables
- [x] **Secondary Track (38 CFR 3.310):** Given service-connected conditions, returns ranked secondary condition exploration recommendations with reasoning, evidence gaps, and citations.
- [x] **Presumptive Track (38 CFR 3.307 / 3.309):** Sourced criteria for Agent Orange and PACT Act airborne hazards/burn pits.
- [x] **Offline Data Model:** Statically served catalog (191 conditions) and local JSON knowledge-base mappings.
- [x] **Public Legal Routes:** Dedicated app pages published at `/privacy` (Zero PII policy) and `/terms` (Scope, legal disclaimers, unverified citations notice).

### Phase 1 Validation Gate Status
- [x] **Clear user problem:** Claim-preparation research aid distinguishing 38 CFR 3.310 (secondary) from 38 CFR 3.307/3.309 (presumptive).
- [x] **No live-data dependency:** Fully operational client-side research tool with offline fallback.
- [x] **Minimal API need:** Local JSON datasets with optional client-side VA sandbox adapter boundary (`src/lib/va/adapter.ts`).
- [x] **Demo readiness:** Repeatable walkthroughs and scenarios (`/scenarios`) validated by green test suites.
- [x] **Public privacy/terms/scope publication:** Live dedicated routes at `/privacy` and `/terms`.
- [x] **Transparent coverage state:** Documentation explicitly states 191 catalog conditions exist (17 published mappings active in UI, 174 draft placeholders excluded from recommendations).

## Phase 2: Sandbox VA API Integration

### Scope

- Add VA API integration in a dedicated adapter layer using sandbox access only.
- Keep core recommendation engine decoupled from external API dependencies.
- Prove workflow value while limiting privacy and authorization complexity.

### Deliverables

- VA developer account setup and sandbox API key management process.
- API connector layer with environment-based configuration.
- Initial integration for selected high-value, lower-sensitivity APIs.
- Request/response logging, error handling, and retry strategy for sandbox calls.
- Internal documentation for sandbox test scenarios and known limitations.

### Acceptance Criteria

- App can call selected sandbox APIs successfully from the integration layer.
- API failures degrade gracefully without breaking core intake/exploration flow.
- Logs provide enough detail to support troubleshooting and demo explanations.
- Sandbox-dependent features can be toggled off without affecting baseline V1.

---

## Phase 3: Production-Access Readiness

### Scope

- Prepare a strong, narrow production-access request tied to a working app.
- Request only the smallest API set required for first real use case.
- Demonstrate policy awareness and responsible data handling.

### Deliverables

- Public-facing product description page.
- Privacy policy and terms of service pages.
- End-user definition (veterans and/or authorized representatives) with use-case boundaries.
- Minimal production API request package and rationale per API.
- Demo script and materials for VA platform review.
- Data-handling, authentication, and rate-limit handling documentation.

### Acceptance Criteria

- A 30–60 minute demo can be run without blockers.
- Production request package is complete and aligned to actual implemented workflows.
- API list is minimal, justified, and mapped to explicit user value.
- Privacy, terms, and scope limitations are clearly published in plain language.

---

## Execution Order

1. Finalize V1 specification and schema.
2. Build responsive intake and results experience.
3. Expand condition knowledge base and explanation outputs.
4. Add rationale/source placeholders and logging.
5. Implement sandbox API adapters and targeted integrations.
6. Prepare production-readiness documents and demo package.
7. Submit production-access request for minimum necessary APIs.

## Validation Gate Before Production Request

### Phase 1 Objective Proof & Artifact Checklist
- [x] **Core Evaluation Logic:** Verified by automated test suite (`npm test`, 79/79 tests passing).
- [x] **Public Legal & Privacy Routes:** Live at `/privacy` (`src/app/privacy/page.tsx`, zero PII policy) and `/terms` (`src/app/terms/page.tsx`, scope, disclaimers, unverified citations notice).
- [x] **Transparent Split Coverage:** 191 total catalog conditions across 14 body systems; 17 published active mappings (`draft: false`) and 174 draft placeholders (`draft: true`) audited via `scripts/kb-authoring-audit.js`.
- [x] **Strict Citation Governance:** Enforced by Zod schemas (`src/lib/kb/schema.ts` & `src/lib/presumptive/schema.ts`) requiring `verified: false` across all knowledge-base and presumptive source references.
- [x] **Accessibility Compliance:** Documented in `docs/accessibility-audit.md` targeting WCAG 2.1 Level AA and validated via semantic test suites (`tests/accessibility-ux-reliability.test.ts`).

FILE: docs/presumptive-track.md
================================================================================

# Presumptive track

## Why this is separate from the knowledge base

Secondary and presumptive service connection are different legal mechanisms.

| | Secondary | Presumptive |
|---|---|---|
| Authority | 38 CFR 3.310 | 38 CFR 3.307, 3.309 |
| Turns on | An existing service-connected condition | Where and when you served |
| Needs a nexus opinion | Yes | No |
| Data | `data/knowledge-base/` | `data/presumptives/` |

Presenting a presumptive condition as a secondary would steer a veteran toward
the harder claim. A Gulf War veteran with asthma does not need a nexus opinion:
asthma diagnosed after service is presumptive for burn pit exposure. That is
why the two tracks have separate data, separate matchers, and separate screens,
with a chooser at `/` that explains the difference before either is used.

## Coverage

Two exposure categories, sourced from va.gov and 38 CFR 3.309(e) on 2026-07-27.

- **Burn pits and other airborne hazards** (PACT Act): 29 conditions, 2
  qualifying service windows, both open ended.
- **Agent Orange and other herbicides**: 19 conditions, 7 qualifying service
  windows, all closed.

## Not covered

These presumptive categories exist and are deliberately out of scope for now.
The results screen says so rather than implying the list is complete.

- Camp Lejeune contaminated water
- Radiation exposure
- Gulf War infectious diseases
- Undiagnosed illness and medically unexplained chronic multi-symptom illness
- Mustard gas and lewisite
- Former prisoners of war
- Tropical diseases
- COVID-19
- Chronic conditions manifesting within a presumptive period after service

## Matching behavior

`matchPresumptives` takes selected locations plus an optional service date
range and returns the categories whose windows overlap.

- Location match is exact against the strings in the data. No fuzzy matching.
- Dates are optional. When omitted, matches rest on location alone and the
  result sets `hasUnconfirmedDates`, which the UI surfaces explicitly.
- Overlap is inclusive at both boundaries. Service on 1990-08-02 matches a
  window opening 1990-08-02.
- Locations that match nothing are reported back, with a note that the veteran
  can still file with evidence linking the condition to service.

## What this screen does not do

It reports which presumptions **may** apply. It is not an eligibility
determination. VA also weighs discharge characterization, the diagnosis itself,
and evidence that can rebut a presumption under 38 CFR 3.307(d).

## Phase 2

The Benefits Reference Data API is the natural source to sync these lists
against, replacing the manual `sourceCheckedOn` process. The adapter belongs in
`src/lib/va/`, and `src/lib/presumptive/match.ts` must keep working when those
calls fail.

FILE: docs/product-spec.md
================================================================================

# Phase 1 Product Specification

Status: draft for review
Scope: Phase 1 only. See `docs/phase-plan.md` for Phases 2 and 3.

## Problem

A veteran service connected for one condition often has no practical way to
learn which related conditions are commonly considered alongside it, or what
evidence a secondary claim would need. That knowledge sits with experienced
representatives and is unevenly distributed.

## Users

- Veterans researching their own claims
- Accredited representatives and VSO staff
- Claim-preparation support staff

## What Phase 1 does

Two research paths, chosen at `/` because they need different evidence.

**Secondary track.** Given one or more service-connected primary conditions,
returns ranked **possible secondary conditions to explore**, each with
reasoning, the evidence a claim on that theory typically needs, and citations
to verify.

**Presumptive track.** Given where and when someone served, returns the
exposure presumptions that may apply and the conditions each covers. See
`docs/presumptive-track.md`.

## What Phase 1 explicitly does not do

- Diagnose, or suggest that any condition is present
- Estimate approval likelihood, ratings, or compensation
- Connect to live VA systems or read a veteran's records
- Store personally identifying information
- Generate or file claim forms

## Functional requirements

### FR-1 Intake

- Secondary intake is a six-step workflow:
  1) diagnosed conditions,
  2) service-connected status,
  3) current ratings,
  4) optional document-upload flag,
  5) grouped suggestions,
  6) evidence review.
- Diagnosed selections come only from catalog condition ids.
- Service-connected step only shows diagnosed selections.
- Ratings step only shows service-connected selections and accepts 0–100 or
  decline.
- The upload step is explicit MVP scaffolding: no document parsing, no medical
  file interpretation, no persisted file contents.
- Steps 2 through 6 are guarded against invalid or incomplete prior-step state.
- Back navigation preserves prior workflow selections through query state so users
  can revise earlier choices without starting over.

### FR-2 Recommendations

Each suggestion shows:

- Condition name
- A qualitative prevalence band: commonly, sometimes, or occasionally explored.
  Never a number, never a percentage.
- Rationale, attributed to each contributing primary condition
- Evidence gaps: what a claim on this theory typically still needs
- Source references, each flagged verified or unverified
- Suggestions grouped by selected service-connected primary condition in the
  workflow UI
- For recognized conditions with no authored mappings, the UI must say they are
  recognized-but-unmapped and that absence implies nothing about claim viability.

Ordering: rank 1 contributes 1.0, rank 2 contributes 0.5, rank n contributes
1/n. Contributions from multiple primaries add, so a suggestion supported by
two conditions outranks a single-source suggestion at the same rank. Ties break
alphabetically. The score is an ordering heuristic and is never displayed.

### FR-3 Multiple primaries

- Suggestions shared across primaries merge on `conditionId`, combining
  rationale, evidence gaps, and citations.
- A condition the user already listed as primary is never suggested as a
  secondary of another primary.
- Duplicate or differently-cased entries for the same condition collapse.

### FR-4 Unmapped and unrecognized conditions

Three outcomes, reported separately, because they mean different things.

- **Mapped**: the condition is in the catalog and has authored research.
- **Unmapped**: the condition is in the catalog but nobody has written its
  secondary research yet. Named explicitly, with a note that absence implies
  nothing about whether a claim is possible.
- **Unrecognized**: the input matched nothing in the catalog.

Additional rules:

- Conditions absent from the catalog are listed back to the user
  explicitly.
- The message states that absence implies nothing about whether a claim is
  possible.
- Lookup is exact match against id, name, and aliases. No fuzzy matching:
  silently resolving "knee" to the wrong entry is worse than returning nothing.

### FR-5 Disclaimers

- A banner appears on intake and results.
- A footer on every page notes that suggestions are not ranked by approval
  likelihood and that citations are unverified starting points.
- Unverified citations carry an inline badge.

### FR-6 Demo scenarios

Three runnable scenarios in `data/demo-scenarios.json`: a single primary, two
primaries with an overlapping suggestion, and a partially recognized intake. A
test asserts each runs end to end.

Scenario compatibility note: `/scenarios` currently targets the legacy
query-based `/results` route for repeatable demos. Keep this route stable until
scenario migration is completed.

### FR-7 Presumptive matching

- Users select service locations and optionally a service date range.
- Matching is exact on location and inclusive on date boundaries.
- Omitted dates produce a match on location alone, flagged explicitly in the UI.
- Unmatched locations are reported back with a note that a claim is still
  possible with supporting evidence.
- Categories out of scope are named on the results screen so the list does not
  read as exhaustive.
- Every category carries source URLs and a `sourceCheckedOn` date.

### FR-8 Draft conditions

- A condition may ship as `draft: true` with an empty `suggestions` array and a
  non-empty `todo` checklist.
- Drafts are validated and version controlled but never resolvable through
  lookup and never listed in the UI.
- Schema rejects a published entry with no suggestions, and rejects a published
  entry with an unresolved todo.

### FR-9 Event logging

Each run records a UUID, timestamp, resolved primary names, unrecognized count,
recommended condition ids, and engine version. Intake free text is never
persisted. SQLite locally when `EVENT_LOG_SQLITE_PATH` is set, otherwise
structured stdout lines.

### FR-10 Evidence review page

- The workflow ends with a structured evidence checklist grouped by
  service-connected primary condition.
- Checklist content is sourced from authored `evidenceGaps` in published
  secondary mappings.
- The page must state that it is organizational support only and does not
  interpret uploaded documents.

## Data model

Three layers, deliberately separate. See `docs/condition-catalog.md`.

- `data/conditions/` is the catalog: condition identity and rating schedule
  citations, one file per body system. 191 conditions, 14 systems.
- `data/knowledge-base/` holds secondary mappings, referencing catalog ids.
- `data/presumptives/` holds exposure categories.

The catalog is the single source of display names, so a label cannot drift
between mapping files. Referential integrity is enforced by tests and by
load-time validation before recommendation logic runs.

JSON in git rather than a database so every change to a mapping or a citation
is a reviewable pull request diff. That audit trail is a Phase 3 compliance
asset.

## Acceptance criteria

Mapped to `docs/phase-plan.md`.

| # | Criterion | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 1 | A user can complete intake and receive ranked exploration suggestions | Met |
| 2 | Every suggestion includes explanation text and at least one source reference | Met, enforced by test |
| 3 | The app works end to end with no live VA data | Met |
| 4 | Disclaimers are visible in intake and results views | Met |
| 5 | At least three demo scenarios run consistently | Met, enforced by test |

## Open items before Phase 2

1. **Citation review workflow (Workflow Enforced / Legal Review In Progress).** All citations ship with `verified: false` and are strictly validated by build/runtime schema checks across both secondary mappings and presumptive source references. Formal subject-matter expert review of individual regulatory citations remains an ongoing operational task governed by `docs/kb-pr-checklist.md`.
2. **Mapping-authoring gaps (In Progress).** 191 catalog conditions have version-controlled knowledge-base files, but coverage is currently split: 17 mappings are published (`draft: false`) and active in UI recommendations, while 174 remain draft placeholders (`draft: true`) awaiting domain authoring.
3. **Accessibility audit (In Progress).** Route-level semantic accessibility contracts are automated in `tests/accessibility-ux-reliability.test.ts` (landmarks, heading structure, scope copy, legal-route discoverability), but formal WCAG 2.1 AA keyboard navigation and screen-reader audit passes (NVDA/VoiceOver) have not yet been conducted.
4. **No authentication (Deliberate).** Intentional for Phase 1/Phase 2 client-side architecture since no veteran PII or intake free text is persisted.

FILE: docs/requirements-matrix.md

# Secondary Condition Finder - Requirements Traceability Matrix

FILE: docs/va-api-access-strategy.md

# VA API Access Strategy

## Objective

Use a sandbox-first approach to validate real product value, then submit a narrow production-access request backed by a working demo, clear end-user definition, and responsible data-handling model.

## Guiding Principles

- Build first, request broad access later.
- Prefer lowest-sensitivity APIs that prove workflow value.
- Request the fewest APIs needed for the first production use case.
- Separate integration adapters from core recommendation logic.
- Treat API credentials and user data with strict confidentiality controls.

---

## Initial API Shortlist (First Production Request Candidate Set)

### Priority 1 (start in sandbox, target earliest production request)

1. **Benefits Reference Data API**
   - Why: Provides structured reference data useful for claims-preparation context.
   - Product value: Improves consistency and official terminology mapping.

2. **VA Forms API**
   - Why: Supports retrieval of official form metadata and references.
   - Product value: Helps users identify and work with correct VA forms.

### Priority 2 (evaluate after Priority 1 is proven)

3. **Benefits Intake API**
   - Why: Supports claim document upload workflows.
   - Product value: Enables document workflow only when app supports an appropriate end-user flow and compliance posture.
   - Gate: Integrate only after document workflow is fully specified and demoable.

### Deferred (not in initial production request unless required)

- Authenticated veteran-specific claims/health APIs with higher privacy and authorization complexity.
- Any API not directly tied to the first bounded production use case.

---

## Sandbox-First Integration Sequence

1. Create VA developer account and obtain sandbox key(s).
2. Confirm APIs and sandbox endpoints needed for Phase 2 experiments.
3. Implement API adapters with environment-specific configuration.
4. Add request observability (request IDs, status, error categories, retry behavior).
5. Build sandbox test scenarios mapped to product user stories.
6. Validate fallback behavior when APIs are unavailable.
7. Capture demo evidence (screens, logs, narrative) for production-access request support.

---

## Production Access Request Package Checklist

- [ ] Short app description and first production use case statement.
- [ ] Defined end-user population (veterans and/or authorized representatives).
- [ ] Exact API list requested with per-API business justification.
- [ ] Data-handling summary (what is stored, where, retention, deletion path).
- [ ] Credential handling approach (secrets management and rotation model).
- [ ] Authentication and authorization flow description (if applicable).
- [ ] Rate-limit and error-handling strategy.
- [ ] Public privacy policy URL.
- [ ] Public terms of service URL.
- [ ] Demo readiness confirmation and walkthrough agenda.

---

## Demo Readiness Checklist (for VA Review)

- [ ] Demo environment stable and repeatable.
- [ ] End-to-end scenario from intake to output runs without manual fixes.
- [ ] Conservative language and disclaimers visible throughout.
- [ ] Recommendation rationale and evidence-gap outputs shown clearly.
- [ ] API-backed features are clearly distinguished from non-API features.
- [ ] Known limitations are explicitly acknowledged.
- [ ] Team can explain why each requested API is necessary and sufficient.

---

## Compliance and Risk Controls

### Data and Privacy

- Collect only data required for the active workflow.
- Avoid storing sensitive user data unless necessary.
- Define retention and deletion behavior before production roll-out.
- Keep privacy and terms documentation aligned with actual behavior.

### Credentials and Access

- Store API keys in secure secrets management, never in source control.
- Restrict key usage by environment and role.
- Rotate credentials on schedule and on incident response triggers.

### Product Framing and Safety

- Position output as informational claim-preparation support only.
- Do not represent the tool as a medical, legal, or eligibility decision engine.
- Avoid deterministic language for uncertain condition relationships.

---

## Decision Rule for Expanding API Scope

Expand beyond initial APIs only when all are true:

1. Existing production APIs are fully utilized in a validated user workflow.
2. New API provides clear incremental user benefit not achievable otherwise.
3. Privacy and authorization implications are documented and acceptable.
4. Demo can show the new API in a bounded, policy-compliant scenario.

If any condition is unmet, defer expansion and continue product hardening.

FILE: generate-dump.js
================================================================================

const fs = require("fs");
const path = require("path");

const outputFile = "codebase-dump.txt";
let bundle = "";

function walk(dir) {
  const files = fs.readdirSync(dir);
  for (const file of files) {
    const fullPath = path.join(dir, file);
    const stat = fs.statSync(fullPath);
    
    // Skip hidden folders, node_modules, build caches, git, logs, and the output file itself
    if (
      file.startsWith(".") ||
      file === "node_modules" ||
      file === ".next" ||
      file === "dist" ||
      file === "build" ||
      file.endsWith(".log") ||
      file.endsWith(".zip") ||
      file === outputFile
    ) {
      continue;
    }
    
    if (stat.isDirectory()) {
      walk(fullPath);
    } else if (
      stat.isFile() &&
      (file.endsWith(".ts") ||
       file.endsWith(".tsx") ||
       file.endsWith(".js") ||
       file.endsWith(".json") ||
       file.endsWith(".md") ||
       file.endsWith(".css"))
    ) {
      bundle += `\n\n================================================================================\n`;
      bundle += `FILE: ${fullPath}\n`;
      bundle += `================================================================================\n\n`;
      bundle += fs.readFileSync(fullPath, "utf8");
    }
  }
}

walk(".");
fs.writeFileSync(outputFile, bundle);
console.log(`✅ Successfully generated ${outputFile}!`);

import type { NextConfig } from "next";

const nextConfig: NextConfig = {
  // The knowledge base is read from disk at runtime, so it must be traced into
  // the standalone output rather than bundled.
  outputFileTracingIncludes: {
    "/**": ["./data/**/*.json"],
  },
  serverExternalPackages: ["better-sqlite3"],
};

export default nextConfig;

================================================================================
FILE: scripts/generate-authoring-tracker.js
================================================================================

/* eslint-disable @typescript-eslint/no-require-imports */
const fs = require("fs");
const path = require("path");

const kbDir = "data/knowledge-base";
const catalogDir = "data/conditions";
const files = fs.readdirSync(kbDir).filter(f => f.endsWith(".json"));

let csvContent = "Condition ID,Body System,Status,Assigned To\n";
let draftCount = 0;

files.forEach(file => {
  const data = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(kbDir, file), "utf8"));
  if (data.draft === true) {
    draftCount++;
    // Look up body system
    let system = "Unknown";
    const catFiles = fs.readdirSync(catalogDir).filter(f => f.endsWith(".json"));
    for (const catFile of catFiles) {
      const catData = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(catalogDir, catFile), "utf8"));
      if (catData.conditions && catData.conditions.some(c => c.id === data.id)) {
        system = catData.systemName || catFile.replace(".json", "");
        break;
      }
    }
    csvContent += `${data.id},${system},DRAFT,\n`;
  }
});

fs.writeFileSync("draft-backlog-tracker.csv", csvContent);
console.log(`✅ Generated draft-backlog-tracker.csv with ${draftCount} pending conditions!`);


================================================================================
FILE: scripts/kb-authoring-audit.js
================================================================================

/* eslint-disable @typescript-eslint/no-require-imports */
const fs = require("fs");
const path = require("path");

const catalogDir = "data/conditions";
const kbDir = "data/knowledge-base";

const catalogFiles = fs.readdirSync(catalogDir).filter(f => f.endsWith(".json"));
const systemSummary = {};

catalogFiles.forEach(catFile => {
  const catData = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(catalogDir, catFile), "utf8"));
  const systemName = catData.systemName || catFile.replace(".json", "");
  const conditionIds = catData.conditions.map(c => c.id);

  let published = 0;
  let drafts = 0;

  conditionIds.forEach(id => {
    const kbPath = path.join(kbDir, `${id}.json`);
    if (fs.existsSync(kbPath)) {
      const kbData = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(kbPath, "utf8"));
      if (kbData.draft === false) published++;
      else drafts++;
    } else {
      drafts++;
    }
  });

  systemSummary[systemName] = { total: conditionIds.length, published, drafts };
});

console.log("================ BODY SYSTEM AUTHORING STATUS ================");
Object.entries(systemSummary).forEach(([sys, stats]) => {
  const pct = stats.total > 0 ? Math.round((stats.published / stats.total) * 100) : 0;
  console.log(`[${sys}] Total: ${stats.total} | Published: ${stats.published} | Drafts: ${stats.draft} (${pct}% complete)`);
});
console.log("==============================================================\n");


================================================================================
FILE: scripts/promote-kb-batch.js
================================================================================

/* eslint-disable @typescript-eslint/no-require-imports */
const fs = require("fs");
const path = require("path");

const kbDir = path.join(process.cwd(), "data/knowledge-base");
const targetId = process.argv[2];

if (!targetId) {
  console.error("Usage: node scripts/promote-kb-batch.js <condition-id>");
  process.exit(1);
}

const filePath = path.join(kbDir, `${targetId}.json`);
if (!fs.existsSync(filePath)) {
  console.error(`Error: Knowledge base file for '${targetId}' not found at ${filePath}`);
  process.exit(1);
}

const data = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(filePath, "utf8"));

let errors = [];
if (!data.suggestions || data.suggestions.length === 0) {
  errors.push("Missing or empty 'suggestions' array.");
}
if (data.todo && data.todo.length > 0) {
  errors.push("Unresolved items remain in the 'todo' checklist.");
}
if (!data.sourceReferences || data.sourceReferences.length === 0) {
  errors.push("At least one source reference citation is required.");
} else {
  data.sourceReferences.forEach((ref, idx) => {
    if (ref.verified !== false) {
      errors.push(`Source reference [${idx}] must have 'verified: false'.`);
    }
  });
}

if (errors.length > 0) {
  console.error(`❌ Validation failed for '${targetId}':`);
  errors.forEach(e => console.error(`   - ${e}`));
  process.exit(1);
}

data.draft = false;
data.todo = [];
fs.writeFileSync(filePath, JSON.stringify(data, null, 2) + "\n");
console.log(`✅ Successfully promoted '${targetId}' from draft to published (draft: false)!`);

const fs = require("fs");
const path = require("path");

console.log("🔍 Running Phase 3 Knowledge Base Batch Validation...");

const trackerPath = "draft-backlog-tracker.csv";
if (!fs.existsSync(trackerPath)) {
  console.error("❌ Backlog tracker CSV not found.");
  process.exit(1);
}

const content = fs.readFileSync(trackerPath, "utf8");
const lines = content.split("\n").filter(Boolean);
console.log(`📊 Total conditions tracked in backlog: ${lines.length - 1}`);

// Verify authoring guide exists
const guidePath = "docs/SME-AUTHORING-GUIDE.md";
if (fs.existsSync(guidePath)) {
  console.log("✅ SME Authoring Guide is present.");
} else {
  console.warn("⚠️ SME Authoring Guide missing!");
}

console.log("✨ Phase 3 validation infrastructure ready for batch authoring!");

import React from 'react';

interface CoverageNoticeProps {
  conditionName: string;
  isUnmapped?: boolean;
}

export function CoverageNotice({ conditionName, isUnmapped = false }: CoverageNoticeProps) {
  if (!isUnmapped) return null;

  return (
    <div 
      className="p-4 my-4 bg-amber-50 border border-amber-200 rounded-lg text-amber-900 shadow-sm"
      role="region"
      aria-label="Coverage Notice"
    >
      <div className="flex items-start">
        <div className="flex-shrink-0">
          <svg className="h-5 w-5 text-amber-500 mt-0.5" viewBox="0 0 20 20" fill="currentColor" aria-hidden="true">
            <path fillRule="evenodd" d="M8.257 3.099c.765-1.36 2.722-1.36 3.486 0l5.58 9.92c.75 1.334-.213 2.98-1.742 2.98H4.42c-1.53 0-2.493-1.646-1.743-2.98l5.58-9.92zM11 13a1 1 0 11-2 0 1 1 0 012 0zm-1-8a1 1 0 00-1 1v3a1 1 0 002 0V6a1 1 0 00-1-1z" clipRule="evenodd" />
          </svg>
        </div>
        <div className="ml-3">
          <h3 className="text-sm font-medium text-amber-800">Recognized Condition ({conditionName}) — Expanding Knowledge Base</h3>
          <p className="mt-1 text-sm text-amber-700">
            This condition is officially recognized in our medical catalog, but detailed secondary mapping rules are currently in development. A missing pre-authored mapping does not rule out the possibility of a valid secondary or aggravated service connection supported by an independent medical opinion (IMO).
          </p>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

import Link from "next/link";

import { DisclaimerBanner, DisclaimerFooter } from "@/components/Disclaimer";

const paths = [
  {
    href: "/secondary",
    title: "A condition related to one I already have",
    mechanism: "Secondary service connection, 38 CFR 3.310",
    body: "You are already service connected for something, and another condition may have been caused or worsened by it. These claims generally need a medical opinion linking the two.",
    cta: "Explore secondary conditions",
  },
  {
    href: "/presumptive",
    title: "A condition tied to where and when I served",
    mechanism: "Presumptive service connection, 38 CFR 3.307 and 3.309",
    body: "For certain exposures, VA presumes your service caused the condition. No linking opinion is needed and no other service-connected condition is required. This is usually the easier path when it applies.",
    cta: "Check presumptive conditions",
  },
];

export default function HomePage() {
  return (
    <main>
      <header className="mb-8">
        <h1 className="text-2xl font-semibold tracking-tight">
          Secondary Condition Finder
        </h1>
        <p className="mt-2 text-sm leading-relaxed text-slate-600">
          A research aid for VA claim preparation. Start with how the condition
          you are researching may connect to your service, since the two paths
          need different evidence.
        </p>
      </header>

      <div className="mb-8">
        <DisclaimerBanner />
      </div>

      <div className="space-y-4">
        {paths.map((path) => (
          <article
            key={path.href}
            className="rounded-lg border border-slate-200 bg-white p-5 shadow-sm"
          >
            <h2 className="text-base font-semibold text-slate-900">
              {path.title}
            </h2>
            <p className="mt-1 text-xs text-slate-500">{path.mechanism}</p>
            <p className="mt-3 text-sm leading-relaxed text-slate-700">
              {path.body}
            </p>
            <Link
              href={path.href}
              className="mt-4 inline-block rounded-md bg-slate-900 px-3.5 py-2 text-sm font-medium text-white hover:bg-slate-700"
            >
              {path.cta}
            </Link>
          </article>
        ))}
      </div>

      <p className="mt-6 text-sm leading-relaxed text-slate-600">
        These paths are not exclusive. A condition can qualify both ways, and
        you can pursue either or both. If you are unsure which applies, an
        accredited representative or VSO can tell you quickly and at no cost.
      </p>

      <p className="mt-6 text-sm text-slate-600">
        Want to see how it works first?{" "}
        <Link href="/scenarios" className="underline hover:text-slate-900">
          Run a demo scenario
        </Link>
        .
      </p>

      <p className="mt-2 text-sm text-slate-600">
        Read our{" "}
        <Link href="/privacy" className="underline hover:text-slate-900">
          privacy policy
        </Link>{" "}
        and{" "}
        <Link href="/terms" className="underline hover:text-slate-900">
          terms of use
        </Link>
        .
      </p>

      <DisclaimerFooter />
    </main>
  );
}

import Link from "next/link";

import { DisclaimerFooter } from "@/components/Disclaimer";
import { ServiceHistoryForm } from "@/components/ServiceHistoryForm";
import { loadExposureCatalog } from "@/lib/presumptive/load";
import { listLocations } from "@/lib/presumptive/match";

export default function PresumptivePage() {
  const locations = listLocations(loadExposureCatalog());

  return (
    <main>
      <Link href="/" className="text-sm underline hover:text-slate-900">
        Back to start
      </Link>

      <header className="mt-3 mb-6">
        <h1 className="text-2xl font-semibold tracking-tight">
          Presumptive conditions by exposure
        </h1>
        <p className="mt-2 text-sm leading-relaxed text-slate-600">
          For some conditions, VA presumes your service caused them based on
          where and when you served. You do not need a nexus opinion or a
          related service-connected condition. This is a different and usually
          easier path than a secondary claim.
        </p>
      </header>

      <div
        role="note"
        className="mb-8 rounded-lg border border-amber-300 bg-amber-50 p-4 text-sm leading-relaxed text-amber-900"
      >
        <p className="font-semibold">This is not an eligibility decision.</p>
        <p className="mt-1">
          This screen shows which presumptions may apply to your service. VA
          also considers your discharge characterization, your diagnosis, and
          evidence that can rebut a presumption. Confirm everything with an
          accredited representative or VSO.
        </p>
      </div>

      <ServiceHistoryForm locations={locations} />

      <DisclaimerFooter />
    </main>
  );
}


================================================================================
FILE: src/app/presumptive/results/page.tsx
================================================================================

import Link from "next/link";

import { DisclaimerFooter } from "@/components/Disclaimer";
import { loadExposureCatalog } from "@/lib/presumptive/load";
import { matchPresumptives, serviceHistorySchema } from "@/lib/presumptive/match";
import type { PresumptiveCondition } from "@/lib/presumptive/schema";

export const dynamic = "force-dynamic";

function toArray(value: string | string[] | undefined): string[] {
  if (!value) return [];
  return (Array.isArray(value) ? value : [value]).map((v) => v.trim()).filter(Boolean);
}

function toDate(value: string | string[] | undefined): string | null {
  const raw = Array.isArray(value) ? value[0] : value;
  return raw && /^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/.test(raw) ? raw : null;
}

function formatDate(iso: string): string {
  return new Date(`${iso}T00:00:00Z`).toLocaleDateString("en-US", {
    year: "numeric",
    month: "long",
    day: "numeric",
    timeZone: "UTC",
  });
}

function groupConditions(
  conditions: PresumptiveCondition[],
): [string, PresumptiveCondition[]][] {
  const groups = new Map<string, PresumptiveCondition[]>();
  for (const condition of conditions) {
    groups.set(condition.group, [...(groups.get(condition.group) ?? []), condition]);
  }
  return [...groups.entries()].sort((a, b) => a[0].localeCompare(b[0]));
}

export default async function PresumptiveResultsPage({
  searchParams,
}: {
  searchParams: Promise<Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>>;
}) {
  const params = await searchParams;

  const parsed = serviceHistorySchema.safeParse({
    locations: toArray(params.location),
    serviceStart: toDate(params.serviceStart),
    serviceEnd: toDate(params.serviceEnd),
  });

  if (!parsed.success) {
    return (
      <main>
        <h1 className="text-2xl font-semibold tracking-tight">
          Nothing to show yet
        </h1>
        <p className="mt-2 text-sm text-slate-600">
          {parsed.error.issues[0]?.message ??
            "Select at least one location where you served."}
        </p>
        <Link
          href="/presumptive"
          className="mt-6 inline-block text-sm underline hover:text-slate-900"
        >
          Back to service history
        </Link>
      </main>
    );
  }

  const result = matchPresumptives(loadExposureCatalog(), parsed.data);

  return (
    <main>
      <Link href="/presumptive" className="text-sm underline hover:text-slate-900">
        Back to service history
      </Link>

      <h1 className="mt-3 text-2xl font-semibold tracking-tight">
        Presumptions that may apply
      </h1>
      <p className="mt-2 text-sm text-slate-600">
        Based on {parsed.data.locations.join(", ")}
        {parsed.data.serviceStart && parsed.data.serviceEnd
          ? `, ${formatDate(parsed.data.serviceStart)} to ${formatDate(parsed.data.serviceEnd)}`
          : ""}
        .
      </p>

      <div
        role="note"
        className="mt-6 rounded-lg border border-amber-300 bg-amber-50 p-4 text-sm leading-relaxed text-amber-900"
      >
        <p className="font-semibold">This is not an eligibility decision.</p>
        <p className="mt-1">
          A presumption removes the need to prove your service caused the
          condition. It does not remove the need for a current diagnosis, and
          VA can rebut a presumption with affirmative evidence.
        </p>
      </div>

      {result.hasUnconfirmedDates && (
        <div className="mt-4 rounded-lg border border-slate-300 bg-white p-4 text-sm text-slate-700">
          <p className="font-medium text-slate-900">Service dates not provided</p>
          <p className="mt-1">
            These matches rest on location alone. Each qualifying window below
            shows its dates. Check yours against your DD-214.
          </p>
        </div>
      )}

      {result.unmatchedLocations.length > 0 && (
        <div className="mt-4 rounded-lg border border-slate-300 bg-white p-4 text-sm text-slate-700">
          <p className="font-medium text-slate-900">No presumption matched</p>
          <p className="mt-1">
            {result.unmatchedLocations.join(", ")}. Either the location has no
            presumption in this app, or your dates fall outside its window. You
            can still file a claim with evidence linking the condition to
            service.
          </p>
        </div>
      )}

      {result.matches.length === 0 ? (
        <p className="mt-6 rounded-lg border border-slate-200 bg-white p-5 text-sm text-slate-700">
          No presumption in this app matches that service history. Other
          presumptive categories exist, including Camp Lejeune, radiation, and
          Gulf War illnesses, which are not yet covered here.
        </p>
      ) : (
        <div className="mt-6 space-y-6">
          {result.matches.map(({ category, matchedPeriods, conditions }) => (
            <article
              key={category.id}
              className="rounded-lg border border-slate-200 bg-white p-5 shadow-sm"
            >
              <h2 className="text-lg font-semibold text-slate-900">
                {category.name}
              </h2>
              <p className="mt-1 text-xs text-slate-500">{category.authority}</p>
              <p className="mt-3 text-sm leading-relaxed text-slate-700">
                {category.summary}
              </p>

              <section className="mt-4 rounded-md bg-slate-50 p-4">
                <h3 className="text-xs font-semibold uppercase tracking-wide text-slate-500">
                  Why this may apply to you
                </h3>
                <ul className="mt-2 space-y-2 text-sm text-slate-700">
                  {matchedPeriods.map(({ period, matchedLocations }) => (
                    <li key={period.id}>
                      <span className="font-medium text-slate-900">
                        {matchedLocations.join(", ")}
                      </span>{" "}
                      &mdash; {formatDate(period.startDate)} to{" "}
                      {period.endDate ? formatDate(period.endDate) : "present"}
                      {period.note && (
                        <span className="mt-0.5 block text-xs text-slate-500">
                          {period.note}
                        </span>
                      )}
                    </li>
                  ))}
                </ul>
              </section>

              <section className="mt-4">
                <h3 className="text-xs font-semibold uppercase tracking-wide text-slate-500">
                  Presumptive conditions ({conditions.length})
                </h3>
                {groupConditions(conditions).map(([group, items]) => (
                  <div key={group} className="mt-3">
                    <h4 className="text-sm font-medium text-slate-900">{group}</h4>
                    <ul className="mt-1.5 space-y-1.5">
                      {items.map((condition) => (
                        <li key={condition.conditionId} className="text-sm text-slate-700">
                          {condition.name}
                          {condition.qualifier && (
                            <span className="mt-0.5 block text-xs text-amber-800">
                              {condition.qualifier}
                            </span>
                          )}
                        </li>
                      ))}
                    </ul>
                  </div>
                ))}
              </section>

              <section className="mt-4 border-t border-slate-200 pt-4">
                <h3 className="text-xs font-semibold uppercase tracking-wide text-slate-500">
                  Sources
                </h3>
                <ul className="mt-2 space-y-1.5">
                  {category.sourceReferences.map((reference) => (
                    <li key={reference.citation} className="text-sm text-slate-700">
                      <a
                        href={reference.url}
                        target="_blank"
                        rel="noreferrer"
                        className="font-mono text-xs text-slate-900 underline"
                      >
                        {reference.citation}
                      </a>
                      {!reference.verified && (
                        <span className="ml-2 rounded bg-amber-100 px-1.5 py-0.5 text-xs text-amber-900">
                          unverified
                        </span>
                      )}
                      <span className="mt-0.5 block text-xs text-slate-500">
                        {reference.note}
                      </span>
                    </li>
                  ))}
                </ul>
                <p className="mt-3 text-xs text-slate-500">
                  List last checked against the VA source on{" "}
                  {formatDate(category.sourceCheckedOn)}. Presumptive lists
                  change; confirm at va.gov before relying on this.
                </p>
              </section>
            </article>
          ))}
        </div>
      )}

      <DisclaimerFooter />
    </main>
  );
}

FILE: src/app/results/page.tsx
================================================================================

import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";

import Link from "next/link";

import { DisclaimerBanner, DisclaimerFooter } from "@/components/Disclaimer";
import { SuggestionCard } from "@/components/SuggestionCard";
import { ENGINE_VERSION, recommend } from "@/lib/engine/recommend";
import { intakeSchema } from "@/lib/engine/types";
import { loadConditionCatalog } from "@/lib/catalog/load";
import { loadKnowledgeBase } from "@/lib/kb/load";
import { getEventLogger } from "@/lib/log";

export const dynamic = "force-dynamic";

function toArray(value: string | string[] | undefined): string[] {
  if (!value) return [];
  return (Array.isArray(value) ? value : [value])
    .flatMap((entry) => entry.split(","))
    .map((entry) => entry.trim())
    .filter(Boolean);
}

export default async function ResultsPage({
  searchParams,
}: {
  searchParams: Promise<Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>>;
}) {
  const params = await searchParams;

  const parsed = intakeSchema.safeParse({
    primaryConditions: toArray(params.primary),
    symptoms: toArray(params.symptoms),
    medications: toArray(params.medications),
    exposures: toArray(params.exposures),
    notes: typeof params.notes === "string" ? params.notes : "",
  });

  if (!parsed.success) {
    return (
      <main>
        <h1 className="text-2xl font-semibold tracking-tight">
          Nothing to show yet
        </h1>
        <p className="mt-2 text-sm text-slate-600">
          Select at least one service-connected condition to see suggestions.
        </p>
        <Link
          href="/secondary"
          className="mt-6 inline-block text-sm underline hover:text-slate-900"
        >
          Back to intake
        </Link>
      </main>
    );
  }

  const result = recommend(
    loadConditionCatalog(),
    loadKnowledgeBase(),
    parsed.data,
  );

  const eventLogger = await getEventLogger();
  eventLogger.record({
    id: randomUUID(),
    createdAt: new Date().toISOString(),
    recognizedPrimaries: result.recognizedPrimaries,
    unrecognizedPrimaryCount: result.unrecognizedPrimaries.length,
    recommendedConditions: result.recommendations.map((r) => r.conditionId),
    engineVersion: ENGINE_VERSION,
  });

  return (
    <main>
      <header className="mb-6">
        <Link href="/secondary" className="text-sm underline hover:text-slate-900">
          Back to intake
        </Link>
        <h1 className="mt-3 text-2xl font-semibold tracking-tight">
          Conditions to explore
        </h1>
        {result.recognizedPrimaries.length > 0 && (
          <p className="mt-2 text-sm text-slate-600">
            Based on {result.recognizedPrimaries.join(", ")}.
          </p>
        )}
      </header>

      <div className="mb-6">
        <DisclaimerBanner />
      </div>

      {result.unmappedPrimaries.length > 0 && (
        <div className="mb-6 rounded-lg border border-slate-300 bg-white p-4 text-sm text-slate-700">
          <p className="font-medium text-slate-900">
            No secondary research written yet
          </p>
          <p className="mt-1">
            {result.unmappedPrimaries.join(", ")}. These are recognized
            conditions, but nobody has authored the secondary-condition
            research for them yet. Nothing below reflects them, and their
            absence says nothing about whether a secondary claim is possible.
          </p>
        </div>
      )}

      {result.unrecognizedPrimaries.length > 0 && (
        <div className="mb-6 rounded-lg border border-slate-300 bg-white p-4 text-sm text-slate-700">
          <p className="font-medium text-slate-900">Not recognized</p>
          <p className="mt-1">
            {result.unrecognizedPrimaries.join(", ")}. We could not match these
            to a known condition. Check the spelling, or search for them on the
            intake page.
          </p>
        </div>
      )}

      {result.recommendations.length === 0 ? (
        <p className="rounded-lg border border-slate-200 bg-white p-5 text-sm text-slate-700">
          No suggestions available for that combination. Try a different
          condition, or check back as the knowledge base grows.
        </p>
      ) : (
        <div className="space-y-4">
          {result.recommendations.map((recommendation, index) => (
            <SuggestionCard
              key={recommendation.conditionId}
              recommendation={recommendation}
              position={index + 1}
            />
          ))}
        </div>
      )}

      <DisclaimerFooter />
    </main>
  );
}

FILE: src/app/scenarios/page.tsx

import Link from "next/link";

import scenarios from "../../../data/demo-scenarios.json";
import { DisclaimerFooter } from "@/components/Disclaimer";

function toQuery(intake: {
  primaryConditions: string[];
  symptoms: string[];
  medications: string[];
  exposures: string[];
  notes: string;
}): string {
  const params = new URLSearchParams();
  for (const condition of intake.primaryConditions) {
    params.append("primary", condition);
  }
  if (intake.symptoms.length) params.set("symptoms", intake.symptoms.join(","));
  if (intake.medications.length) {
    params.set("medications", intake.medications.join(","));
  }
  if (intake.exposures.length) {
    params.set("exposures", intake.exposures.join(","));
  }
  if (intake.notes) params.set("notes", intake.notes);
  return params.toString();
}

export default function ScenariosPage() {
  return (
    <main>
      <Link href="/" className="text-sm underline hover:text-slate-900">
        Back to start
      </Link>

      <h1 className="mt-3 text-2xl font-semibold tracking-tight">
        Demo scenarios
      </h1>
      <p className="mt-2 text-sm text-slate-600">
        Repeatable walkthroughs for demos. Each one runs the same engine as the
        intake form.
      </p>

      <div className="mt-6 space-y-4">
        {scenarios.map((scenario) => (
          <article
            key={scenario.id}
            className="rounded-lg border border-slate-200 bg-white p-5 shadow-sm"
          >
            <h2 className="text-base font-semibold text-slate-900">
              {scenario.title}
            </h2>
            <p className="mt-1 text-sm leading-relaxed text-slate-600">
              {scenario.description}
            </p>
            <p className="mt-3 text-xs text-slate-500">
              Conditions: {scenario.intake.primaryConditions.join(", ")}
            </p>
            <Link
              href={`/results?${toQuery(scenario.intake)}`}
              className="mt-4 inline-block rounded-md bg-slate-900 px-3.5 py-2 text-sm font-medium text-white hover:bg-slate-700"
            >
              Run scenario
            </Link>
          </article>
        ))}
      </div>

      <DisclaimerFooter />
    </main>
  );
}

import Link from "next/link";

import { DisclaimerBanner, DisclaimerFooter } from "@/components/Disclaimer";
import { loadConditionCatalog } from "@/lib/catalog/load";
import { loadKnowledgeBase } from "@/lib/kb/load";
import { mappedConditionIds } from "@/lib/kb/lookup";
import { parseDiagnosedPrefill } from "@/lib/secondary-workflow/state";

export const dynamic = "force-dynamic";

export default async function DiagnosedConditionsPage({
  searchParams,
}: {
  searchParams: Promise<Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>>;
}) {
  const catalog = loadConditionCatalog();
  const mapped = mappedConditionIds(loadKnowledgeBase());
  const conditions = [...catalog].sort((a, b) => a.name.localeCompare(b.name));
  const params = await searchParams;
  const selectedDiagnosed = new Set(parseDiagnosedPrefill(params, catalog));

  return (
    <main>
      <Link href="/secondary" className="text-sm underline hover:text-slate-900">
        Back to workflow overview
      </Link>

      <header className="mt-3 mb-6">
        <p className="text-xs font-semibold uppercase tracking-wide text-slate-500">Step 1 of 6</p>
        <h1 className="mt-1 text-2xl font-semibold tracking-tight">Select diagnosed conditions</h1>
        <p className="mt-2 text-sm leading-relaxed text-slate-600">
          Choose every diagnosis you want to evaluate in this session. The list comes from the
          condition catalog and does not infer anything from free text.
        </p>
      </header>

      <div className="mb-6">
        <DisclaimerBanner />
      </div>

      <form action="/secondary/service-connected" method="get">
        <fieldset>
          <legend className="text-sm font-semibold text-slate-900">Diagnosed conditions</legend>
          <p className="mt-1 text-sm text-slate-600">
            Select one or more conditions, then continue. A “no mappings yet” label means the
            condition is recognized, but secondary research has not been authored yet.
          </p>

          <ul className="mt-3 max-h-[28rem] divide-y divide-slate-100 overflow-y-auto rounded-lg border border-slate-200 bg-white">
            {conditions.map((condition) => (
              <li key={condition.id}>
                <label className="flex cursor-pointer items-start gap-3 px-3 py-2.5 hover:bg-slate-50">
                  <input
                    type="checkbox"
                    name="diagnosed"
                    value={condition.id}
                    defaultChecked={selectedDiagnosed.has(condition.id)}
                    className="mt-1 h-4 w-4 rounded border-slate-300"
                  />
                  <span className="text-sm text-slate-700">
                    <span className="block font-medium text-slate-900">{condition.name}</span>
                    <span className="block text-xs text-slate-500">{condition.bodySystem}</span>
                  </span>
                  {mapped.has(condition.id) ? (
                    <span className="ml-auto shrink-0 rounded-full bg-slate-100 px-2 py-0.5 text-xs text-slate-700">
                      research available
                    </span>
                  ) : (
                    <span className="ml-auto shrink-0 rounded-full bg-amber-50 px-2 py-0.5 text-xs text-amber-800">
                      no mappings yet
                    </span>
                  )}
                </label>
              </li>
            ))}
          </ul>
        </fieldset>

        <button
          type="submit"
          className="mt-6 rounded-md bg-slate-900 px-4 py-2.5 text-sm font-medium text-white hover:bg-slate-700"
        >
          Continue to service-connected status
        </button>
      </form>

      <DisclaimerFooter />
    </main>
  );
}
